     1	# Goal-loop + dual-worktree pipeline (Layers 2+3, v1 plan)
     2	
     3	Status: APPROVED — Gate 1 passed at iteration 4 of 5 (2026-07-18,
     4	codex/gpt-5.5 @ xhigh, `degraded: model-fallback`).
     5	
     6	## Context
     7	
     8	baton v1 (Layer 1, cross-harness usage-limit failover) is complete: Gate-2
     9	passed, a 40-finding surface audit folded, and the live e2e handoff loop
    10	verified through the real installed plugin. Layers 2 and 3 automate the
    11	development methodology itself on top of that foundation: a goal-driven loop
    12	with review gates (Layer 2) and a dual-worktree adversarial write/review
    13	pipeline (Layer 3).
    14	
    15	## Locked decisions (Saksham, 2026-07-18)
    16	
    17	1. **Packaging**: inside the baton repo — new commands `baton loop` and
    18	   `baton pipeline`, sharing the role matrix, resolver, doctor, and
    19	   checkpoint/handoff machinery.
    20	2. **Layer 3 swap cadence**: per subtask — writer and reviewer swap seats
    21	   every subtask; the planner role plans and the merger role performs the
    22	   final adversarial merge (concrete chains live in `baton.config.json`).
    23	3. **Layer 2 smoke gate**: one staged gate after planning — the loop must
    24	   produce a minimal end-to-end slice (build + run + one real assertion) and
    25	   get explicit approval before the full run; the 5-iteration cap governs
    26	   every gate.
    27	4. **Driver**: a supervisor CLI — spawns headless role children
    28	   (`claude -p` / `codex exec`), parses verdicts, and invokes baton handoff
    29	   automatically on limit death. Survives the operator's session ending.
    30	
    31	## Design
    32	
    33	### Loop spec
    34	
    35	`loop.json` at repo root (schema `baton/loop@1`), scaffolded by
    36	`baton loop init "<goal>"`:
    37	
    38	- `goal`, `constraints[]` (falsifiable acceptance criteria — the loop's exit
    39	  condition), `smoke` ({cmd, expect} — the staged-gate slice definition),
    40	- `phases` — an ordered list of `{id, role}` entries: `id` is a free label
    41	  for progress display; `role` MUST be a matrix role ID (validation fails on
    42	  any unknown role before a child spawns). Default:
    43	  plan(`planner`) → gate-1(`plan-reviewer`) → smoke — itself a TDD mini-cycle
    44	  per the repo contract: smoke-tests(`test-author`) →
    45	  smoke-verify(`test-verifier`) → smoke-build(`implementer`) → the human
    46	  smoke gate — → per-subtask cycle (`test-author` → `test-verifier` →
    47	  `implementer` → `subtask-reviewer`) → gate-2(`final-reviewer-a`,
    48	  `final-reviewer-b`) → done,
    49	- `budgets` ({iterationCap: 5, perRoleTimeoutMin, maxChildrenPerPhase}),
    50	- roles resolve through the existing `baton.config.json` matrix — never
    51	  hardcoded models.
    52	
    53	### Role-matrix additions (Gate-1 iteration 1, finding 1)
    54	
    55	The loop references roles by ID only; every referenced role must exist in the
    56	matrix or `loop.json` validation fails (`unknown role '<id>'`, exit 2, before
    57	any child spawns). Four roles are ADDED to `baton.config.json` +
    58	`templates/baton.config.json.tpl` in the first implementation task:
    59	
    60	- `subtask-reviewer` — per-subtask diff review inside the loop cycle.
    61	- `merger` — the only role that writes main in pipeline mode; adversarial
    62	  re-check before merge.
    63	- `worker-a`, `worker-b` — the two pipeline seats. Subtask N assigns
    64	  writer = seat (N mod 2), reviewer = the other seat; the SEATS swap, and each
    65	  seat's concrete platform/model comes from its configured chain via the
    66	  resolver. No vendor or model name appears in this plan, child prompts, or
    67	  loop code — chains live in config only.
    68	
    69	### `baton loop` (Layer 2)
    70	
    71	- `baton loop init <goal>` — scaffold spec + plan template; idempotent,
    72	  dry-run-able, same two-phase plan/apply discipline as `baton init`.
    73	- `baton loop run [--approve-smoke <token>]` — the state machine. State lives
    74	  in gitignored `.handoff/loop/state.json` + `journal.ndjson` (same atomic
    75	  write + journal replay machinery as the bundle; loop position is also
    76	  checkpointed into the handoff bundle so a limit death carries it).
    77	
    78	### Root + ownership discipline (Gate-1 iteration 1, finding 2)
    79	
    80	One authoritative root: the MAIN repo root where `loop.json` lives. The
    81	supervisor is the SOLE writer of `.handoff/loop/` and of the main root's
    82	handoff bundle; every supervisor-driven baton invocation passes explicit
    83	`--root <mainRoot>` and a supervisor-owned stable session hint
    84	(`--session loop-<runId>` — a NEW flag added to `checkpoint`; `receive`
    85	already has it), so the bundle has exactly one stable owner.
    86	
    87	**Child-hook policy (Gate-1 iteration 2, finding 1; iteration 3,
    88	finding 1)** — children never touch OR read any bundle, by construction: the
    89	supervisor sets `BATON_SUPERVISED_CHILD=1` in every child's environment, and
    90	EVERY baton hook command — the hook entrypoints, `baton checkpoint`, and
    91	`baton session-start` (which would otherwise read the bundle and inject
    92	pending-handoff resume context into the child) — quietly no-ops (exit 0,
    93	empty output) when it is set. This covers
    94	Layer-2 children running in the MAIN root (where root discovery would
    95	otherwise find the supervisor-owned bundle and the foreign-session guard
    96	would reject their stable hints) and pipeline children in worktrees alike;
    97	narrative checkpointing for supervised work is the supervisor's job, driven
    98	by parsed child results. Contract tests: (a) Layer-2 — two concurrent
    99	main-root children with distinct stable session hints and live hooks →
   100	zero bundle mutations, zero foreign-session rejections, supervisor
   101	`--session` checkpoints land; (b) pipeline — same from two worktrees while
   102	the supervisor writes loop state.
   103	- Each role invocation is a headless child under the supervisor:
   104	  - claude-code roles: `claude -p "<role prompt>" --permission-mode
   105	    acceptEdits --allowedTools ...` (allowlist per role: reviewers read-only).
   106	  - codex roles: `codex exec -C <root> -s <sandbox> --model <m> -c
   107	    model_reasoning_effort=<e> "<prompt>" </dev/null` (stdin closed — the
   108	    stdin-hang class is a known field failure).
   109	  - Verdict contract: children end with the structured tail
   110	    (`VERDICT: APPROVED|APPROVED_WITH_NOTES|BLOCKED` + FINDINGS) already used
   111	    by every review gate; the supervisor parses exactly that.
   112	
   113	### Child supervision contract (Gate-1 iteration 1, finding 7)
   114	
   115	`children.mjs` owns the full child lifecycle, not just argv assembly:
   116	
   117	- Spawn detached in its OWN process group; stdin closed at spawn (the
   118	  codex-exec stdin-hang class); per-role timeout → SIGTERM to the group, 10 s
   119	  grace, then SIGKILL to the group (no zombie grandchildren; reap verified in
   120	  tests with a child that forks a sleeper).
   121	- Full child output streams to `.handoff/loop/children/<child-id>.log` with a
   122	  byte cap (head preserved, middle truncated with a marker — the verdict tail
   123	  is at the END so the tail is never truncated).
   124	- **Verdict parsing is region-bounded (prompt-echo defense)**: only the
   125	  child's FINAL message region is parsed — claude-code children run with a
   126	  structured output format and the parser reads the result field; codex
   127	  children are parsed strictly AFTER the last `tokens used` marker. The
   128	  prompt region (which quotes `VERDICT:` in its instructions) is never
   129	  scanned. Multiple tails in the final region → the LAST one wins.
   130	- An unparseable, absent, or truncated verdict is BLOCKED-with-escalation,
   131	  never APPROVED; a child that exits after making edits but before a verdict
   132	  parks the subtask with its worktree preserved for inspection.
   133	- A generic nonzero exit classifies as `other-error` (existing classifier
   134	  rule) → the loop retries once, then parks; it NEVER triggers limit failover
   135	  without limit-specific evidence.
   136	- Fixture-driven tests: prompt echo, multiple verdict tails, truncated
   137	  transcript, timeout kill/reap, zombie grandchild, non-limit nonzero exit.
   138	
   139	### Supervisor lifetime and recovery (Gate-1 iteration 2, finding 5)
   140	
   141	"Survives the operator's session ending" is a defined mechanism, not a hope:
   142	
   143	- `baton loop run` is FOREGROUND by default. `--detach` re-launches the
   144	  supervisor in its own session (`setsid`), output to
   145	  `.handoff/loop/supervisor.log`, and returns.
   146	- A run lock `.handoff/loop/supervisor.lock` carries {pid, process
   147	  start-time, host, runId} — the same provably-dead machinery as the
   148	  Layer-1 bundle lock. A second `loop run` against a LIVE supervisor is
   149	  refused; against a provably-dead one it recovers.
   150	- The state machine is journal-replayable: recovery replays
   151	  `journal.ndjson`, marks any in-flight child of the dead supervisor as
   152	  interrupted (its process group is gone or killed on adoption), and either
   153	  continues from the last completed step or parks with an escalation note —
   154	  never re-runs a completed gate iteration.
   155	- Acceptance adds a supervisor-death test: kill the supervisor (and its
   156	  launching session) mid-subtask → a fresh `loop run` recovers via the
   157	  journal and continues or parks; no orphaned children survive (process
   158	  groups reaped on adoption).
   159	
   160	- **Smoke gate (staleness-proof — Gate-1 iteration 1, finding 8; TDD order —
   161	  iteration 2, finding 2)**: after gate-1, the smoke slice is built as a TDD
   162	  mini-cycle (test-author writes the failing smoke assertion → test-verifier
   163	  approves it → implementer writes only enough to pass it — the repo TDD
   164	  contract applies to the slice, not just to subtasks); the supervisor then
   165	  runs `smoke.cmd`, records output, and STOPS, writing
   166	  `.handoff/loop/SMOKE-REVIEW.md` plus `smoke-approval.json` containing an
   167	  approval token = digest over {loop-state digest, smoke.cmd, output digest,
   168	  HEAD, content-sensitive git digest (existing snapshot machinery), child
   169	  assignment}. Resuming requires `baton loop run --approve-smoke <token>`;
   170	  the supervisor recomputes the digest first and REFUSES a drifted token
   171	  (anything changed since the human looked) — it parks for a fresh smoke run
   172	  instead. No full-run budget is spent before a valid approval.
   173	- **Limit failover (the baton integration — Gate-1 iteration 1, finding 3)**:

    15	// RED/acceptance — real-fs, real-git end-to-end proofs for the Layer-2/3 loop &
    16	// pipeline (subtask e2e-acceptance). Source: plan §"Acceptance constraints" 1–6.

    18	// over mkdtemp repos with a real io (mirrors tests/integration/e2e-failover +
    19	// loop-children-spawn). Children are in-process async fns injected via

    24	// ACCUMULATES its avoid list across failovers and parks on the second death

    28	const SKIP_WIN = process.platform === 'win32';
    29	const pexec = promisify(realExecFile);

    36	const tmpDirs = [];
    37	after(() => { for (const d of tmpDirs) rmSync(d, { recursive: true, force: true }); });
    38	function mkTmp(prefix) { const d = mkdtempSync(join(tmpdir(), prefix)); tmpDirs.push(d); return d; }
    39	
    40	function git(cwd, args, env) {
    41	  const r = spawnSync('git', args, { cwd, encoding: 'utf8', env: env ? { ...process.env, ...env } : process.env });
    42	  if (r.status !== 0) throw new Error(`git ${args.join(' ')} failed: ${r.stderr || r.stdout}`);
    43	  return r.stdout;
    44	}

    46	const CONFIG = {
    47	  schema: 'baton/config@1',
    48	  roles: {
    49	    planner: ['codex/plan-m@xhigh'],
    50	    // Two-platform chain so a limit failover can move claude-code -> codex (-> back).
    51	    implementer: ['claude-code/cc-impl', 'codex/cx-impl@xhigh'],
    52	    'test-author': ['codex/ta@xhigh'],
    53	    'test-verifier': ['codex/tv@xhigh'],
    54	    'plan-reviewer': ['codex/pr@xhigh'],
    55	    'final-reviewer-a': ['codex/fra@xhigh'],
    56	    'final-reviewer-b': ['codex/frb@xhigh'],
    57	    // Pipeline seats — codex so worker/merger argv carry --model and merges FF.
    58	    'worker-a': ['codex/wa@xhigh'],
    59	    'worker-b': ['codex/wb@xhigh'],
    60	    merger: ['codex/mg@xhigh'],
    61	    'subtask-reviewer': ['codex/sr@xhigh'],
    62	  },
    63	  platforms: { 'claude-code': {}, codex: {}, cursor: {} },
    64	  defaults: { 'claude-code': 'claude-fable-5', codex: 'gpt-5.6-sol', cursor: 'composer' },
    65	};

    67	/** A real git repo, sole-author config, one initial commit, gitignore seeded. */
    68	function initRepo(prefix, spec) {
    69	  // Canonicalize: git resolves worktree paths to their realpath (on macOS the
    70	  // tmpdir is a /var -> /private/var symlink), so the repo root must be the
    71	  // realpath too or `git worktree list` paths won't match worktreePaths().
    72	  const cwd = nodeFs.realpathSync(mkTmp(prefix));
    73	  git(cwd, ['init', '-q', '-b', 'main']);
    74	  git(cwd, ['config', 'user.name', NAME]);
    75	  git(cwd, ['config', 'user.email', EMAIL]);
    76	  git(cwd, ['config', 'commit.gpgsign', 'false']);
    77	  // Pre-seed BOTH ignore lines so setupWorktrees is a no-op (constraint 5: the
    78	  // only tracked change would be the .worktrees/ line — pre-seeding removes it).
    79	  nodeFs.writeFileSync(join(cwd, '.gitignore'), '.handoff/\n.worktrees/\n');
    80	  nodeFs.writeFileSync(join(cwd, 'baton.config.json'), JSON.stringify(CONFIG, null, 2));
    81	  nodeFs.writeFileSync(join(cwd, 'README.md'), '# demo\n');
    82	  if (spec) nodeFs.writeFileSync(join(cwd, 'loop.json'), JSON.stringify(spec, null, 2) + '\n');
    83	  git(cwd, ['add', '.']);
    84	  git(cwd, ['commit', '-q', '-m', 'initial']);
    85	  return cwd;
    86	}
    87	
    88	function makeRealIo(cwd, { superviseChild, now = NOW } = {}) {
    89	  const out = [];
    90	  const err = [];
    91	  let tokenN = 0;
    92	  return {
    93	    cwd,
    94	    env: { ...process.env },
    95	    stdin: '',
    96	    stdout: { write: (s) => (out.push(String(s)), true) },
    97	    stderr: { write: (s) => (err.push(String(s)), true) },
    98	    fs: nodeFs,
    99	    execFile: (cmd, args = [], opts = {}) => pexec(cmd, args, { ...opts, encoding: 'utf8' }),
   100	    now: () => now,
   101	    host: 'e2e-host',
   102	    pid: process.pid,
   103	    startTime: Math.round(performance.timeOrigin),
   104	    processAlive: (pid) => { try { process.kill(pid, 0); return true; } catch (e) { return /** @type {any} */ (e).code === 'EPERM'; } },
   105	    platform: process.platform,
   106	    newFencingToken: () => `tok-${(tokenN += 1)}`,
   107	    superviseChild,
   108	    stdoutText: () => out.join(''),
   109	    stderrText: () => err.join(''),
   110	  };
   111	}
   112	
   113	const valAfter = (arr, flag) => { const i = arr.indexOf(flag); return i >= 0 ? arr[i + 1] : undefined; };
   114	const mainLog = (cwd) => git(cwd, ['log', 'main', '--format=%an|%ae|%cn|%ce|%s']).trim().split('\n').filter(Boolean);
   115	const historyFreezes = (cwd) => {
   116	  const dir = bundlePaths(cwd).historyDir;
   117	  if (!nodeFs.existsSync(dir)) return [];
   118	  return nodeFs.readdirSync(dir).filter((n) => n.endsWith('.json')).map((n) => ({ name: n, data: JSON.parse(nodeFs.readFileSync(join(dir, n), 'utf8')) }));
   119	};

   121	// ===========================================================================
   122	describe('e2e — pipeline over REAL git: two subtasks, real merges, no residue (constraints 3, 5)', () => {
   123	  const pipeSpec = { schema: 'baton/loop@1', goal: 'e2e pipeline', constraints: [], budgets: { iterationCap: 5, perRoleTimeoutMin: 30, maxChildrenPerPhase: 10 }, subtasks: [{ id: 't1', title: 'first' }, { id: 't2', title: 'second' }] };
   124	
   125	  // Writers (workspace-write) do REAL git commits in their seat worktree using
   126	  // the identity the supervisor injected via the child env (childSpec.env);
   127	  // reviewers + merger (read-only) just APPROVE.
   128	  function realWriterRunner() {
   129	    let n = 0;
   130	    return async (childSpec, opts) => {
   131	      const args = (childSpec.args ?? []).map(String);
   132	      if (valAfter(args, '-s') === 'workspace-write') {
   133	        const seat = valAfter(args, '-C');
   134	        n += 1;
   135	        nodeFs.writeFileSync(join(seat, `work-${n}.txt`), `change ${n}\n`);
   136	        git(seat, ['add', '.']);
   137	        git(seat, ['commit', '-q', '-m', `subtask change ${n}`], childSpec.env);
   138	      }
   139	      return { timedOut: false, exitCode: 0, verdict: 'APPROVED', findings: '', logPath: opts.logPath };
   140	    };
   141	  }
   142	
   143	  // Wrap the REAL execFile so we can prove the supervisor scanned the exact
   144	  // per-subtask ranges at merge time (finding 2 — no vacuous main..main).
   145	  async function runPipeline(prefix) {
   146	    const cwd = initRepo(prefix, pipeSpec);
   147	    const io = makeRealIo(cwd, { superviseChild: realWriterRunner() });
   148	    const gitLogRanges = [];
   149	    const realExec = io.execFile;
   150	    io.execFile = (cmd, args = [], opts = {}) => {
   151	      if (cmd === 'git' && args[0] === 'log') {
   152	        const range = args.find((a) => /\.\./.test(String(a)));
   153	        if (range) gitLogRanges.push(String(range));
   154	      }
   155	      return realExec(cmd, args, opts);
   156	    };
   157	    const { run } = await import('../../core/src/cli.mjs');
   158	    const code = await run(['pipeline', 'run'], io);
   159	    return { cwd, io, code, gitLogRanges };
   160	  }
   161	
   162	  it('completes with two sole-author real merges on main; the merge-time attribution scan ran on each subtask range (constraint 3)', { skip: SKIP_WIN }, async () => {
   163	    const { cwd, io, code, gitLogRanges } = await runPipeline('baton-e2e-pipe-');
   164	    assert.equal(code, 0, `pipeline should complete; stderr: ${io.stderrText()}`);
   165	    assert.equal((await loadLoopState(cwd, io)).state.status, LOOP_STATUS.DONE, 'the pipeline reached done');
   166	
   167	    const subtaskCommits = mainLog(cwd).filter((l) => /subtask change/.test(l));
   168	    assert.equal(subtaskCommits.length, 2, `both subtasks merged to main; got: ${JSON.stringify(mainLog(cwd))}`);
   169	    for (const line of mainLog(cwd)) {
   170	      const [an, ae, cn, ce] = line.split('|');
   171	      assert.equal(an, NAME); assert.equal(ae, EMAIL);
   172	      assert.equal(cn, NAME); assert.equal(ce, EMAIL);
   173	    }
   174	    assert.doesNotMatch(git(cwd, ['log', 'main', '--format=%B']), /co-authored-by|generated (with|by)|\u{1F916}/iu, 'no forbidden attribution on main');
   175	
   176	    // The REAL merge gate scanned exactly the two per-subtask ranges (not main..main).
   177	    assert.ok(gitLogRanges.includes('main..baton/wt-a/subtask-t1'), `merge scanned the subtask-t1 range; saw ${JSON.stringify(gitLogRanges)}`);
   178	    assert.ok(gitLogRanges.includes('main..baton/wt-b/subtask-t2'), `merge scanned the subtask-t2 range; saw ${JSON.stringify(gitLogRanges)}`);
   179	    assert.ok(!gitLogRanges.includes('main..main'), 'no vacuous main..main scan');
   180	
   181	    assert.match(git(cwd, ['branch', '--list', 'baton/wt-*']), /baton\/wt-a\/|baton\/wt-b\//, 'wt-* branches existed during the run');
   182	  });
   183	
   184	  it('teardown + purge .handoff leaves a PLAIN git repo — no worktrees, no baton/wt-* branches (constraint 5)', { skip: SKIP_WIN }, async () => {
   185	    const { cwd, io } = await runPipeline('baton-e2e-pipe-td-');
   186	    await teardownWorktrees(cwd, io);
   187	    rmSync(join(cwd, '.handoff'), { recursive: true, force: true });
   188	    assert.equal(git(cwd, ['status', '--porcelain']).trim(), '', 'the working tree is clean');
   189	    assert.equal(git(cwd, ['worktree', 'list', '--porcelain']).split('\n\n').filter(Boolean).length, 1, 'only the main worktree remains');
   190	    // FINDING (constraint 5): teardown deletes only each worktree's CURRENT branch,
   191	    // leaving orphaned baton/wt-*/base branches after seats switch to subtask
   192	    // branches — residue. Constraint 5 requires a plain repo, so this must be [].
   193	    assert.equal(git(cwd, ['branch', '--list', 'baton/wt-*']).trim(), '', 'no baton/wt-* branches remain');
   194	  });
   195	});

   226	  const loopSpec = { schema: 'baton/loop@1', goal: 'e2e failover', constraints: [], budgets: { iterationCap: 5, perRoleTimeoutMin: 30, maxChildrenPerPhase: 10 }, phases: [{ id: 'p0', role: 'implementer' }, { id: 'p1', role: 'implementer' }] };

   228	  // A scripted loop runner: returns the next result and writes its log so the
   229	  // supervisor classifies the transcript from the child's LOG file.
   230	  function scriptedRunner(script) {
   231	    const queue = [...script];
   232	    const calls = [];
   233	    const fn = async (childSpec, opts) => {
   234	      calls.push({ command: childSpec.command, args: (childSpec.args ?? []).map(String) });
   235	      const r = queue.shift() ?? { verdict: 'APPROVED' };
   236	      if (typeof r.logContent === 'string' && opts.logPath) {
   237	        nodeFs.mkdirSync(opts.logPath.slice(0, opts.logPath.lastIndexOf('/')), { recursive: true });
   238	        nodeFs.writeFileSync(opts.logPath, r.logContent);
   239	      }
   240	      return { timedOut: false, exitCode: r.exitCode ?? 0, verdict: r.verdict ?? 'APPROVED', findings: '', logPath: opts.logPath };
   241	    };
   242	    fn.calls = calls;
   243	    return fn;
   244	  }
   245	
   246	  it('SINGLE limit death (A→B): seals usage-limit, receives onto the other platform, relaunches, completes', { skip: SKIP_WIN }, async () => {
   247	    const cwd = initRepo('baton-e2e-limitA-', loopSpec);
   248	    const runner = scriptedRunner([
   249	      { verdict: 'BLOCKED', exitCode: 1, logContent: `implementing...\n${CC_LIMIT}\n` }, // claude-code child dies
   250	      { verdict: 'APPROVED' }, // relaunched codex child succeeds
   251	      { verdict: 'APPROVED' }, // phase 1
   252	    ]);
   253	    const io = makeRealIo(cwd, { superviseChild: runner });
   254	    const code = await cmdLoop(['run'], io);
   255	    assert.equal(code, 0, `single failover should complete; stderr: ${io.stderrText()}`);
   256	
   257	    // A sealed finalize freeze with reasonClass usage-limit exists.
   258	    const fin = historyFreezes(cwd).find((f) => /\.finalize\.json$/.test(f.name));
   259	    assert.ok(fin && fin.data.handoff.status === 'sealed' && fin.data.handoff.reasonClass === 'usage-limit', 'the dead session was sealed usage-limit');

   139	### Supervisor lifetime and recovery (Gate-1 iteration 2, finding 5)
   140	
   141	"Survives the operator's session ending" is a defined mechanism, not a hope:
   142	
   143	- `baton loop run` is FOREGROUND by default. `--detach` re-launches the
   144	  supervisor in its own session (`setsid`), output to
   145	  `.handoff/loop/supervisor.log`, and returns.
   146	- A run lock `.handoff/loop/supervisor.lock` carries {pid, process
   147	  start-time, host, runId} — the same provably-dead machinery as the
   148	  Layer-1 bundle lock. A second `loop run` against a LIVE supervisor is
   149	  refused; against a provably-dead one it recovers.
   150	- The state machine is journal-replayable: recovery replays
   151	  `journal.ndjson`, marks any in-flight child of the dead supervisor as
   152	  interrupted (its process group is gone or killed on adoption), and either
   153	  continues from the last completed step or parks with an escalation note —
   154	  never re-runs a completed gate iteration.
   155	- Acceptance adds a supervisor-death test: kill the supervisor (and its
   156	  launching session) mid-subtask → a fresh `loop run` recovers via the
   157	  journal and continues or parks; no orphaned children survive (process
   158	  groups reaped on adoption).
   159	
   160	- **Smoke gate (staleness-proof — Gate-1 iteration 1, finding 8; TDD order —
   161	  iteration 2, finding 2)**: after gate-1, the smoke slice is built as a TDD
   162	  mini-cycle (test-author writes the failing smoke assertion → test-verifier
   163	  approves it → implementer writes only enough to pass it — the repo TDD
   164	  contract applies to the slice, not just to subtasks); the supervisor then
   165	  runs `smoke.cmd`, records output, and STOPS, writing
   166	  `.handoff/loop/SMOKE-REVIEW.md` plus `smoke-approval.json` containing an
   167	  approval token = digest over {loop-state digest, smoke.cmd, output digest,
   168	  HEAD, content-sensitive git digest (existing snapshot machinery), child
   169	  assignment}. Resuming requires `baton loop run --approve-smoke <token>`;
   170	  the supervisor recomputes the digest first and REFUSES a drifted token
   171	  (anything changed since the human looked) — it parks for a fresh smoke run
   172	  instead. No full-run budget is spent before a valid approval.
   173	- **Limit failover (the baton integration — Gate-1 iteration 1, finding 3)**:
   174	  an exact transaction, not a loose chain. On child death: (1) reap the
   175	  process group and freeze the transcript; (2) classify via `baton detect`;
   176	  non-limit → the retry/park path above. On a limit classification:
   177	  (3) checkpoint the loop position INTO THE BUNDLE (supervisor
   178	  `checkpoint --session loop-<runId>`) so the seal carries it and the receipt
   179	  token binds it; (4) seal via `finalize --reason-class usage-limit --root
   180	  <mainRoot>` (an already-dead/unsealed bundle takes the degraded-open path
   181	  receive already supports); (5) refresh probes; (6) `receive --prepare` then
   182	  `--commit` back-to-back with IDENTICAL intake flags, the supervisor session
   183	  hint, and the same explicit root, with NO intervening writes; (7) resolver
   184	  output (with the dead entry avoided) picks the relaunch platform+model;
   185	  (8) relaunch the child with the resume prompt. A stale-token rejection
   186	  triggers ONE automatic re-prepare/commit retry; a second failure parks.
   187	  Between prepare and commit the failover holds the loop transaction lock and
   188	  writes NOTHING — `.handoff/loop/` writes are not token-bound inputs, so
   189	  this is enforced by construction and ASSERTED in tests (fs spy: zero
   190	  `.handoff/loop/` writes inside the window), not assumed. Drift-rejection
   191	  tests inject the inputs the token actually binds: git change and probe
   192	  refresh between prepare and commit → stale-token → one retry path. Plus:
   193	  open-bundle (unsealed) limit death; the full seal→receive→relaunch happy
   194	  path.
   195	- **Model-level failover (Gate-1 iteration 1, finding 4)**: the resolver
   196	  gains entry-level avoidance — `avoidEntries: [{platform, model}]` alongside
   197	  the existing platform-level `avoid[]` — and the classifier gains a
   198	  `model-unavailable` signature class (distinct from usage-limit; seeded with
   199	  the verified account-tier rejection string). A model rejected at spawn adds
   200	  its {platform, model} entry to avoidEntries and re-resolves: other models
   201	  on the SAME platform remain selectable. Named test: first chain entry
   202	  rejected as model-unavailable → the next entry on the same platform is
   203	  selected, with no platform-wide avoidance.
   204	  All platforms/entries exhausted → the loop parks with a resume-at hint
   205	  instead of dying.
   206	- **Caps**: every gate loops at most 5 iterations; on exhaustion the loop
   207	  parks and escalates (writes `.handoff/loop/ESCALATION.md`, notifies) — never
   208	  a 6th run.
   209	
   210	### `baton pipeline` (Layer 3 — a loop preset)
   211	
   212	- `baton pipeline run` = `loop run` with the dual-worktree subtask cycle:
   213	  - Two persistent worktrees `.worktrees/wt-a`, `.worktrees/wt-b`
   214	    (`git worktree add`); `pipeline init` ensure-lines `.worktrees/` into
   215	    `.gitignore` (same idempotent machinery as the `.handoff/` line).
   216	  - Subtask N: the writer seat implements on a namespaced branch
   217	    (`baton/wt-<seat>/subtask-N`) in its worktree; the reviewer seat reviews
   218	    the diff from the OTHER worktree (fresh checkout, fresh context). Seats
   219	    swap every subtask: worker-a writes / worker-b reviews → worker-b writes /
   220	    worker-a reviews → … (concrete models per seat come from the role
   221	    matrix — see Role-matrix additions).
   222	  - Verdict flow: APPROVED → the merger role adversarially re-checks and
   223	    merges into main (the ONLY writer to main); BLOCKED → writer fixes,
   224	    ≤5 iterations, then escalate. After each merge both worktrees
   225	    fast-forward.
   226	
   227	  **Worktree transaction layer (Gate-1 iteration 1, finding 5)** —
   228	  `worktrees.mjs` treats every git operation as a guarded transaction:
   229	  - Branch namespace `baton/wt-*/…` is loop-owned; anything else is never
   230	    touched.
   231	  - Preflight before each child: worktree exists in `git worktree list`,
   232	    index clean, HEAD on the expected branch — a dirty or drifted worktree
   233	    parks the subtask instead of spawning.
   234	  - Postflight after each child: HEAD still on the expected branch, main
   235	    unmoved by the child — violation parks with the evidence.
   236	  - Merges run under a supervisor-held merge lock; worktree sync after merge
   237	    is ff-only (a non-ff sync is a corruption signal → park); merge conflicts
   238	    park + escalate, never auto-resolved.
   239	  - Stale metadata self-heals: a raw-deleted worktree directory is detected
   240	    (`git worktree list` vs fs), pruned (`git worktree prune`), and re-added;

    55	     8	baton v1 (Layer 1, cross-harness usage-limit failover) is complete: Gate-2

    58	    11	development methodology itself on top of that foundation: a goal-driven loop
    59	    12	with review gates (Layer 2) and a dual-worktree adversarial write/review
    60	    13	pipeline (Layer 3).
    61	    14	
    62	    15	## Locked decisions (Saksham, 2026-07-18)
    63	    16	
    64	    17	1. **Packaging**: inside the baton repo — new commands `baton loop` and
    65	    18	   `baton pipeline`, sharing the role matrix, resolver, doctor, and
    66	    19	   checkpoint/handoff machinery.
    67	    20	2. **Layer 3 swap cadence**: per subtask — writer and reviewer swap seats
    68	    21	   every subtask; the planner role plans and the merger role performs the
    69	    22	   final adversarial merge (concrete chains live in `baton.config.json`).
    70	    23	3. **Layer 2 smoke gate**: one staged gate after planning — the loop must
    71	    24	   produce a minimal end-to-end slice (build + run + one real assertion) and
    72	    25	   get explicit approval before the full run; the 5-iteration cap governs
    73	    26	   every gate.
    74	    27	4. **Driver**: a supervisor CLI — spawns headless role children
    75	    28	   (`claude -p` / `codex exec`), parses verdicts, and invokes baton handoff
    76	    29	   automatically on limit death. Survives the operator's session ending.
    77	    30	
    78	    31	## Design
    79	    32	
    80	    33	### Loop spec
    81	    34	
    82	    35	`loop.json` at repo root (schema `baton/loop@1`), scaffolded by
    83	    36	`baton loop init "<goal>"`:
    84	    37	
    85	    38	- `goal`, `constraints[]` (falsifiable acceptance criteria — the loop's exit
    86	    39	  condition), `smoke` ({cmd, expect} — the staged-gate slice definition),
    87	    40	- `phases` — an ordered list of `{id, role}` entries: `id` is a free label
    88	    41	  for progress display; `role` MUST be a matrix role ID (validation fails on
    89	    42	  any unknown role before a child spawns). Default:
    90	    43	  plan(`planner`) → gate-1(`plan-reviewer`) → smoke — itself a TDD mini-cycle
    91	    44	  per the repo contract: smoke-tests(`test-author`) →
    92	    45	  smoke-verify(`test-verifier`) → smoke-build(`implementer`) → the human
    93	    46	  smoke gate — → per-subtask cycle (`test-author` → `test-verifier` →
    94	    47	  `implementer` → `subtask-reviewer`) → gate-2(`final-reviewer-a`,
    95	    48	  `final-reviewer-b`) → done,
    96	    49	- `budgets` ({iterationCap: 5, perRoleTimeoutMin, maxChildrenPerPhase}),
    97	    50	- roles resolve through the existing `baton.config.json` matrix — never
    98	    51	  hardcoded models.
    99	    52	
   100	    53	### Role-matrix additions (Gate-1 iteration 1, finding 1)
   101	    54	
   102	    55	The loop references roles by ID only; every referenced role must exist in the
   103	    56	matrix or `loop.json` validation fails (`unknown role '<id>'`, exit 2, before
   104	    57	any child spawns). Four roles are ADDED to `baton.config.json` +
   105	    58	`templates/baton.config.json.tpl` in the first implementation task:
   106	    59	
   107	    60	- `subtask-reviewer` — per-subtask diff review inside the loop cycle.
   108	    61	- `merger` — the only role that writes main in pipeline mode; adversarial
   109	    62	  re-check before merge.
   110	    63	- `worker-a`, `worker-b` — the two pipeline seats. Subtask N assigns
   111	    64	  writer = seat (N mod 2), reviewer = the other seat; the SEATS swap, and each
   112	    65	  seat's concrete platform/model comes from its configured chain via the
   113	    66	  resolver. No vendor or model name appears in this plan, child prompts, or
   114	    67	  loop code — chains live in config only.
   115	    68	
   116	    69	### `baton loop` (Layer 2)
   117	    70	
   118	    71	- `baton loop init <goal>` — scaffold spec + plan template; idempotent,
   119	    72	  dry-run-able, same two-phase plan/apply discipline as `baton init`.
   120	    73	- `baton loop run [--approve-smoke <token>]` — the state machine. State lives
   121	    74	  in gitignored `.handoff/loop/state.json` + `journal.ndjson` (same atomic
   122	    75	  write + journal replay machinery as the bundle; loop position is also
   123	    76	  checkpointed into the handoff bundle so a limit death carries it).
   124	    77	
   125	    78	### Root + ownership discipline (Gate-1 iteration 1, finding 2)
   126	    79	
   127	    80	One authoritative root: the MAIN repo root where `loop.json` lives. The
   128	    81	supervisor is the SOLE writer of `.handoff/loop/` and of the main root's
   129	    82	handoff bundle; every supervisor-driven baton invocation passes explicit
   130	    83	`--root <mainRoot>` and a supervisor-owned stable session hint
   131	    84	(`--session loop-<runId>` — a NEW flag added to `checkpoint`; `receive`
   132	    85	already has it), so the bundle has exactly one stable owner.
   133	    86	
   134	    87	**Child-hook policy (Gate-1 iteration 2, finding 1; iteration 3,
   135	    88	finding 1)** — children never touch OR read any bundle, by construction: the
   136	    89	supervisor sets `BATON_SUPERVISED_CHILD=1` in every child's environment, and
   137	    90	EVERY baton hook command — the hook entrypoints, `baton checkpoint`, and
   138	    91	`baton session-start` (which would otherwise read the bundle and inject
   139	    92	pending-handoff resume context into the child) — quietly no-ops (exit 0,
   140	    93	empty output) when it is set. This covers
   141	    94	Layer-2 children running in the MAIN root (where root discovery would
   142	    95	otherwise find the supervisor-owned bundle and the foreign-session guard
   143	    96	would reject their stable hints) and pipeline children in worktrees alike;
   144	    97	narrative checkpointing for supervised work is the supervisor's job, driven
   145	    98	by parsed child results. Contract tests: (a) Layer-2 — two concurrent
   146	    99	main-root children with distinct stable session hints and live hooks →
   147	   100	zero bundle mutations, zero foreign-session rejections, supervisor
   148	   101	`--session` checkpoints land; (b) pipeline — same from two worktrees while
   149	   102	the supervisor writes loop state.
   150	   103	- Each role invocation is a headless child under the supervisor:
   151	   104	  - claude-code roles: `claude -p "<role prompt>" --permission-mode
   152	   105	    acceptEdits --allowedTools ...` (allowlist per role: reviewers read-only).
   153	   106	  - codex roles: `codex exec -C <root> -s <sandbox> --model <m> -c
   154	   107	    model_reasoning_effort=<e> "<prompt>" </dev/null` (stdin closed — the
   155	   108	    stdin-hang class is a known field failure).
   156	   109	  - Verdict contract: children end with the structured tail
   157	   110	    (`VERDICT: APPROVED|APPROVED_WITH_NOTES|BLOCKED` + FINDINGS) already used
   158	   111	    by every review gate; the supervisor parses exactly that.
   159	   112	
   160	   113	### Child supervision contract (Gate-1 iteration 1, finding 7)
   161	   114	
   162	   115	`children.mjs` owns the full child lifecycle, not just argv assembly:
   163	   116	
   164	   117	- Spawn detached in its OWN process group; stdin closed at spawn (the
   165	   118	  codex-exec stdin-hang class); per-role timeout → SIGTERM to the group, 10 s
   166	   119	  grace, then SIGKILL to the group (no zombie grandchildren; reap verified in
   167	   120	  tests with a child that forks a sleeper).
   168	   121	- Full child output streams to `.handoff/loop/children/<child-id>.log` with a
   169	   122	  byte cap (head preserved, middle truncated with a marker — the verdict tail
   170	   123	  is at the END so the tail is never truncated).
   171	   124	- **Verdict parsing is region-bounded (prompt-echo defense)**: only the
   172	   125	  child's FINAL message region is parsed — claude-code children run with a
   173	   126	  structured output format and the parser reads the result field; codex
   174	   127	  children are parsed strictly AFTER the last `tokens used` marker. The
   175	   128	  prompt region (which quotes `VERDICT:` in its instructions) is never
   176	   129	  scanned. Multiple tails in the final region → the LAST one wins.
   177	   130	- An unparseable, absent, or truncated verdict is BLOCKED-with-escalation,
   178	   131	  never APPROVED; a child that exits after making edits but before a verdict
   179	   132	  parks the subtask with its worktree preserved for inspection.
   180	   133	- A generic nonzero exit classifies as `other-error` (existing classifier
   181	   134	  rule) → the loop retries once, then parks; it NEVER triggers limit failover
   182	   135	  without limit-specific evidence.
   183	   136	- Fixture-driven tests: prompt echo, multiple verdict tails, truncated
   184	   137	  transcript, timeout kill/reap, zombie grandchild, non-limit nonzero exit.
   185	   138	
   186	   139	### Supervisor lifetime and recovery (Gate-1 iteration 2, finding 5)
   187	   140	
   188	   141	"Survives the operator's session ending" is a defined mechanism, not a hope:
   189	   142	
   190	   143	- `baton loop run` is FOREGROUND by default. `--detach` re-launches the
   191	   144	  supervisor in its own session (`setsid`), output to
   192	   145	  `.handoff/loop/supervisor.log`, and returns.
   193	   146	- A run lock `.handoff/loop/supervisor.lock` carries {pid, process
   194	   147	  start-time, host, runId} — the same provably-dead machinery as the
   195	   148	  Layer-1 bundle lock. A second `loop run` against a LIVE supervisor is
   196	   149	  refused; against a provably-dead one it recovers.
   197	   150	- The state machine is journal-replayable: recovery replays
   198	   151	  `journal.ndjson`, marks any in-flight child of the dead supervisor as
   199	   152	  interrupted (its process group is gone or killed on adoption), and either
   200	   153	  continues from the last completed step or parks with an escalation note —
   201	   154	  never re-runs a completed gate iteration.
   202	   155	- Acceptance adds a supervisor-death test: kill the supervisor (and its
   203	   156	  launching session) mid-subtask → a fresh `loop run` recovers via the
   204	   157	  journal and continues or parks; no orphaned children survive (process
   205	   158	  groups reaped on adoption).
   206	   159	
   207	   160	- **Smoke gate (staleness-proof — Gate-1 iteration 1, finding 8; TDD order —
   208	   161	  iteration 2, finding 2)**: after gate-1, the smoke slice is built as a TDD
   209	   162	  mini-cycle (test-author writes the failing smoke assertion → test-verifier
   210	   163	  approves it → implementer writes only enough to pass it — the repo TDD
   211	   164	  contract applies to the slice, not just to subtasks); the supervisor then
   212	   165	  runs `smoke.cmd`, records output, and STOPS, writing
   213	   166	  `.handoff/loop/SMOKE-REVIEW.md` plus `smoke-approval.json` containing an
   214	   167	  approval token = digest over {loop-state digest, smoke.cmd, output digest,
   215	   168	  HEAD, content-sensitive git digest (existing snapshot machinery), child
   216	   169	  assignment}. Resuming requires `baton loop run --approve-smoke <token>`;
   217	   170	  the supervisor recomputes the digest first and REFUSES a drifted token
   218	   171	  (anything changed since the human looked) — it parks for a fresh smoke run
   219	   172	  instead. No full-run budget is spent before a valid approval.
   220	   173	- **Limit failover (the baton integration — Gate-1 iteration 1, finding 3)**:

   258	### Module layout (all under `core/src/loop/`)
   259	
   260	`spec.mjs` (load/validate loop.json incl. role-existence check) · `state.mjs`

   263	detect-on-exit) · `failover.mjs` (the limit-death transaction) ·

   268	signature table gains the `model-unavailable` class; `checkpoint` gains a
   269	`--session <hint>` flag (supervisor identity), and the
   270	`BATON_SUPERVISED_CHILD` no-op guard lands in the hook entrypoints,
   271	`checkpoint`, AND `session-start` (every hook-invoked command).
   272	
   273	### Acceptance constraints (the loop's exit condition)
   274	
   275	1. On a fixture repo, `baton loop run` drives a toy goal end-to-end headlessly:
   276	   plan → gate-1 → smoke slice AS A TDD MINI-CYCLE (failing smoke assertion
   277	   authored and verifier-approved BEFORE the slice implementation — order
   278	   asserted from the journal) → parked for approval → (approved) → 2 subtasks
   279	   TDD → gate-2 → done, with review artifacts under `reviews/` in the fixture.
   280	   Layer-2 children run in the main root with live hooks and neither mutate
   281	   NOR read the supervisor-owned bundle: under `BATON_SUPERVISED_CHILD=1`,
   282	   hook commands on all three platforms produce zero bundle writes AND zero
   283	   session-start resume context (both asserted).
   284	2. Simulated limit death (child exits with a verbatim limit banner) mid-subtask
   285	   → the loop seals, remaps the role off the dead platform, resumes on the
   286	   other harness, and completes the subtask. Chained A→B→A tested. Separately:
   287	   a model-unavailable rejection at spawn selects the NEXT entry on the same
   288	   platform (no platform-wide avoidance).
   289	3. `baton pipeline run` completes 2 subtasks with alternating writer/reviewer
   290	   across the two worktrees; the attribution range scan passes for main AND
   291	   both worker branch ranges (child commits included) — sole author, zero AI
   292	   attribution; reviewer children run read-only.
   293	4. The 5-cap parks + escalates on every gate (unit-forced exhaustion test);
   294	   no gate ever runs a 6th iteration. Killing the supervisor (and its
   295	   launching session) mid-subtask → a fresh `loop run` recovers via the
   296	   journal and continues or parks, with no orphaned children.
   297	5. Deleting `.handoff/` and `.worktrees/` leaves the fixture repo exactly as
   298	   a plain git repo (no residue outside gitignored trees).
   299	6. All existing 878 tests stay green; the new suite covers spec validation,
   300	   state-machine transitions (unit, injected io), child verdict parsing
   301	   (fixture transcripts), failover (fake children), and worktree discipline.
   302	
   303	### Top risks
   304	
   305	1. **Headless permission surfaces** — Claude Code children need permission
   306	   flags that stay inside the sandboxed repo; reviewers get read-only
   307	   allowlists. Mitigation: per-role allowlist table + spike task 0.
   308	2. **Verdict-parse drift** — models sometimes wrap the tail; the
   309	   region-bounded parser (Child supervision contract) takes the LAST tail in
   310	   the final-message region only and treats an unparseable one as
   311	   BLOCKED-with-escalation, never as APPROVED.
   312	3. **Cost runaway** — per-phase child caps + iteration caps + the smoke gate
   313	   before any full-run spend; every child's token/duration logged to the loop
   314	   journal.
   315	4. **Worktree state divergence** — only the merger writes main; workers are
   316	   branch-jailed; ff-sync after each merge; `baton pipeline doctor` checks
   317	   worktree health.
   318	5. **Cross-platform child API drift** — the spawn contract lives in ONE module
   319	   (children.mjs) with per-platform fixtures, same seam discipline as
   320	   normalize.mjs.
   321	
   322	## Out of scope for this milestone
   323	
   324	Multi-repo loops; parallel subtask fan-out (>1 writer at once); cloud/cron
   325	driving (the supervisor is local-first; scheduled runs are a later layer);
   326	LLM-judged smoke verdicts (the smoke gate is a human gate by decision 3).

     1	/**
     2	 * Guarded worktree transaction layer (Layer 3) — every git operation is a
     3	 * checked transaction: namespaced branches only, pre/postflight assertions
     4	 * around each child, merges under a supervisor merge lock gated on a

    10	import { ensureIgnoreLine } from '../scaffold/gitignore.mjs';
    11	import { atomicWriteJson, ensureDir } from '../util/fsx.mjs';
    12	
    13	export const WORKTREE_BRANCH_RE = /^baton\/wt-(a|b)\//;
    14	
    15	const SEATS = /** @type {const} */ (['a', 'b']);
    16	const SOLE_AUTHOR = { name: 'SakshamUboweja', email: 'ssakshamu@gmail.com' };

    18	// Mirrors the doctor git-guard trailer discipline (core/src/commands/doctor.mjs).
    19	const TRAILER_PATTERNS = [
    20	  /co-authored-by:[^\n]*\b(claude|gpt|copilot|codex)\b/i,
    21	  /generated (with|by)[^\n]*\bclaude\b/i,
    22	  /\u{1F916}/u,
    23	];
    24	
    25	// One declared log format — per-commit fields NUL-delimited in this exact
    26	// order (sha, author name/email, committer name/email, body), records
    27	// RS-terminated. The parser below consumes exactly this shape.
    28	const LOG_FORMAT = '%H%x00%an%x00%ae%x00%cn%x00%ce%x00%B%x1e';
    29	
    30	/** @param {string} root */
    31	export function worktreePaths(root) {
    32	  const dir = `${root}/.worktrees`;
    33	  /** @type {Record<string, string>} */
    34	  const seats = { a: `${dir}/wt-a`, b: `${dir}/wt-b` };
    35	  return { dir, seats };
    36	}
    37	
    38	/** @param {string} seat */
    39	const seatBranch = (seat) => `baton/wt-${seat}/base`;
    40	
    41	/** @param {any} io @param {string} cwd @param {string[]} args */
    42	async function git(io, cwd, args) {
    43	  return io.execFile('git', args, { cwd });
    44	}
    45	
    46	/** Parse `git worktree list --porcelain` into [{path, branch}]. @param {string} out */
    47	function parseWorktreeList(out) {
    48	  /** @type {Array<{path: string, branch: string | null}>} */
    49	  const entries = [];
    50	  for (const block of String(out).split(/\n\n+/)) {
    51	    const path = block.match(/^worktree (.+)$/m)?.[1];
    52	    if (!path) continue;
    53	    const branch = block.match(/^branch refs\/heads\/(.+)$/m)?.[1] ?? null;
    54	    entries.push({ path, branch });
    55	  }
    56	  return entries;
    57	}
    58	
    59	/**
    60	 * Ensure both seat worktrees exist on their namespaced branches and that
    61	 * `.worktrees/` is gitignored. Idempotent: an existing seat is left alone.
    62	 * @param {string} root @param {any} io
    63	 * @returns {Promise<{ok: boolean, worktrees: Array<{seat: string, path: string, branch: string}>, gitignoreChanged: boolean}>}
    64	 */
    65	export async function setupWorktrees(root, io) {
    66	  const p = worktreePaths(root);
    67	
    68	  const gitignorePath = `${root}/.gitignore`;
    69	  const existing = io.fs.existsSync(gitignorePath) ? io.fs.readFileSync(gitignorePath, 'utf8') : '';
    70	  const ensured = ensureIgnoreLine(existing, '.worktrees/');
    71	  if (ensured.changed) io.fs.writeFileSync(gitignorePath, ensured.text);
    72	
    73	  const listed = parseWorktreeList((await git(io, root, ['worktree', 'list', '--porcelain'])).stdout);
    74	  /** @type {Array<{seat: string, path: string, branch: string}>} */
    75	  const worktrees = [];
    76	  for (const seat of SEATS) {
    77	    const path = p.seats[seat];
    78	    const present = listed.find((e) => e.path === path);
    79	    if (present) {
    80	      worktrees.push({ seat, path, branch: present.branch ?? seatBranch(seat) });
    81	      continue;
    82	    }
    83	    const branch = seatBranch(seat);
    84	    await git(io, root, ['worktree', 'add', '-b', branch, path]);
    85	    worktrees.push({ seat, path, branch });
    86	  }
    87	  return { ok: true, worktrees, gitignoreChanged: ensured.changed };
    88	}
    89	
    90	/**
    91	 * Preflight before a child runs in a seat: the worktree is listed, its index
    92	 * is clean, and HEAD sits on the expected branch. Refusals name the failed
    93	 * check and never issue destructive git.
    94	 * @param {string} root @param {{seat: string, branch: string}} target @param {any} io
    95	 * @returns {Promise<{ok: true} | {ok: false, check: string, refusal: string}>}
    96	 */
    97	export async function preflightWorktree(root, target, io) {
    98	  const path = worktreePaths(root).seats[target.seat];
    99	  const listed = parseWorktreeList((await git(io, root, ['worktree', 'list', '--porcelain'])).stdout);
   100	  if (!listed.some((e) => e.path === path)) {
   101	    return { ok: false, check: 'missing-worktree', refusal: `worktree ${path} is not listed by git — run setup or self-heal first` };
   102	  }
   103	  const status = (await git(io, path, ['status', '--porcelain'])).stdout.trim();
   104	  if (status.length > 0) {
   105	    return { ok: false, check: 'dirty-index', refusal: `worktree ${path} has a dirty index/working tree — a clean seat is required before a child runs` };
   106	  }
   107	  const head = (await git(io, path, ['rev-parse', '--abbrev-ref', 'HEAD'])).stdout.trim();
   108	  if (head !== target.branch) {
   109	    return { ok: false, check: 'wrong-branch', refusal: `worktree ${path} HEAD is on '${head}', expected branch '${target.branch}'` };
   110	  }
   111	  return { ok: true };
   112	}
   113	
   114	/**
   115	 * Postflight after a child: HEAD still on the expected branch (checked in the
   116	 * seat cwd) and main unmoved from the pre-child capture.
   117	 * @param {string} root @param {{seat: string, branch: string, mainSha: string}} target @param {any} io
   118	 * @returns {Promise<{ok: true} | {ok: false, check: string, refusal: string}>}
   119	 */
   120	export async function postflightWorktree(root, target, io) {
   121	  const path = worktreePaths(root).seats[target.seat];
   122	  const head = (await git(io, path, ['rev-parse', '--abbrev-ref', 'HEAD'])).stdout.trim();
   123	  if (head !== target.branch) {
   124	    return { ok: false, check: 'head-drift', refusal: `the child left HEAD on '${head}' instead of the expected branch '${target.branch}'` };
   125	  }
   126	  const main = (await git(io, root, ['rev-parse', 'main'])).stdout.trim();
   127	  if (main !== target.mainSha) {
   128	    return { ok: false, check: 'main-moved', refusal: `main moved under the child (was ${target.mainSha}, now ${main}) — only the merger writes main` };
   129	  }
   130	  return { ok: true };
   131	}
   132	
   133	/**
   134	 * Full-range attribution scan over main..branch — the merge gate. Every
   135	 * commit must be authored AND committed by the sole author, with no forbidden
   136	 * AI-attribution trailer in the body. The range is never windowed.
   137	 * @param {string} root @param {string} branch @param {any} io
   138	 * @returns {Promise<{ok: true} | {ok: false, offending: string, reason: string}>}
   139	 */
   140	export async function attributionScan(root, branch, io) {
   141	  const out = (await git(io, root, ['log', `main..${branch}`, `--format=${LOG_FORMAT}`])).stdout;
   142	  for (const record of String(out).split('\x1e')) {
   143	    if (record.trim().length === 0) continue;
   144	    const [sha, an, ae, cn, ce, body = ''] = record.replace(/^\n/, '').split('\x00');
   145	    if (an !== SOLE_AUTHOR.name || ae !== SOLE_AUTHOR.email) {
   146	      return { ok: false, offending: sha, reason: `commit ${sha} has a foreign author identity (${an} <${ae}>) — sole-author is required` };
   147	    }
   148	    if (cn !== SOLE_AUTHOR.name || ce !== SOLE_AUTHOR.email) {
   149	      return { ok: false, offending: sha, reason: `commit ${sha} has a foreign committer identity (${cn} <${ce}>) — sole-author is required` };
   150	    }
   151	    if (TRAILER_PATTERNS.some((re) => re.test(body))) {
   152	      return { ok: false, offending: sha, reason: `commit ${sha} carries a forbidden AI-attribution trailer (co-authored/generated-with/emoji) in its message` };
   153	    }
   154	  }
   155	  return { ok: true };
   156	}
   157	
   158	const mergeLockPath = (/** @type {string} */ root) => `${root}/.handoff/loop/merge.lock`;
   159	
   160	/**
   161	 * Merge an approved subtask branch into main — one guarded transaction:
   162	 * acquire the merge lock (a held lock refuses), gate on the attribution scan,
   163	 * merge at the repo root, abort-and-park on conflict, then ff-only sync both
   164	 * seats. The lock is released on EVERY exit path after acquisition.
   165	 * @param {string} root @param {{branch: string}} input @param {any} io
   166	 * @returns {Promise<{ok: true, branch: string} | {ok: false, reason: string}>}
   167	 */
   168	export async function mergeSubtask(root, input, io) {
   169	  const lock = mergeLockPath(root);
   170	  if (io.fs.existsSync(lock)) {
   171	    return { ok: false, reason: `the merge lock at ${lock} is held by another merger — refusing a concurrent merge` };
   172	  }
   173	  ensureDir(io.fs, `${root}/.handoff/loop`);
   174	  atomicWriteJson(io.fs, lock, { host: io.host, pid: io.pid, at: io.now() });
   175	  try {
   176	    const scan = await attributionScan(root, input.branch, io);
   177	    if (scan.ok !== true) return { ok: false, reason: scan.reason };
   178	
   179	    try {
   180	      await git(io, root, ['merge', input.branch]);
   181	    } catch (err) {
   182	      // Conflict (or any merge failure): abort, never auto-resolve — park.
   183	      try {
   184	        await git(io, root, ['merge', '--abort']);
   185	      } catch {
   186	        // Nothing to abort — the failure predates a merge state.
   187	      }
   188	      const msg = /** @type {any} */ (err)?.stdout || /** @type {any} */ (err)?.message || String(err);
   189	      return { ok: false, reason: `merge conflict — aborted and parked for the operator: ${String(msg).split('\n')[0]}` };
   190	    }
   191	
   192	    const seats = worktreePaths(root).seats;
   193	    for (const seat of SEATS) {
   194	      try {
   195	        await git(io, seats[seat], ['merge', '--ff-only', 'main']);
   196	      } catch (err) {
   197	        const msg = /** @type {any} */ (err)?.stderr || /** @type {any} */ (err)?.message || String(err);
   198	        return { ok: false, reason: `worktree wt-${seat} could not fast-forward to main — corruption signal, parked: ${String(msg).split('\n')[0]}` };
   199	      }
   200	    }
   201	    return { ok: true, branch: input.branch };
   202	  } finally {
   203	    try {
   204	      if (io.fs.existsSync(lock)) io.fs.unlinkSync(lock);
   205	    } catch {
   206	      // Best effort — a leaked lock is visible and operator-recoverable.
   207	    }
   208	  }
   209	}
   210	
   211	/**
   212	 * Self-heal a raw-deleted seat: git still lists it but the directory is gone
   213	 * — prune the stale metadata and re-add on the same branch.
   214	 * @param {string} root @param {{seat: string, branch: string}} target @param {any} io
   215	 * @returns {Promise<{ok: boolean, healed: boolean}>}
   216	 */
   217	export async function selfHealWorktree(root, target, io) {
   218	  const path = worktreePaths(root).seats[target.seat];
   219	  const listed = parseWorktreeList((await git(io, root, ['worktree', 'list', '--porcelain'])).stdout);
   220	  const stale = listed.some((e) => e.path === path) && !io.fs.existsSync(path);
   221	  if (!stale) return { ok: true, healed: false };
   222	  await git(io, root, ['worktree', 'prune']);
   223	  await git(io, root, ['worktree', 'add', path, target.branch]);
   224	  return { ok: true, healed: true };
   225	}
   226	
   227	/**
   228	 * Tear down BOTH seats: remove the worktrees and delete their namespaced
   229	 * branches. Anything outside .worktrees/wt-* or baton/wt-* is never touched,
   230	 * so a teardown leaves the repo a plain git repo (acceptance constraint 5).
   231	 * @param {string} root @param {any} io
   232	 * @returns {Promise<{ok: boolean, removed: string[]}>}
   233	 */
   234	export async function teardownWorktrees(root, io) {
   235	  const p = worktreePaths(root);
   236	  const listed = parseWorktreeList((await git(io, root, ['worktree', 'list', '--porcelain'])).stdout);
   237	  /** @type {string[]} */
   238	  const removed = [];
   239	  for (const seat of SEATS) {
   240	    const entry = listed.find((e) => e.path === p.seats[seat]);
   241	    if (!entry) continue;
   242	    await git(io, root, ['worktree', 'remove', '--force', p.seats[seat]]);
   243	    if (entry.branch && WORKTREE_BRANCH_RE.test(entry.branch)) {
   244	      await git(io, root, ['branch', '-D', entry.branch]);
   245	    }
   246	    removed.push(seat);
   247	  }
   248	  return { ok: true, removed };
   249	}

   252	 * Branch jail: only baton/wt-* branches may ever be deleted through this
   253	 * layer. An out-of-namespace ref refuses without touching git.
   254	 * @param {string} root @param {string} branch @param {any} io
   255	 * @returns {Promise<{ok: true} | {ok: false, error: string}>}

   259	    return { ok: false, error: `refused: '${branch}' is outside the baton/wt- namespace — the loop never deletes non-loop branches` };
   260	  }
   261	  await git(io, root, ['branch', '-D', branch]);
   262	  return { ok: true };
   263	}

    31	/**
    32	 * @param {string[]} args @param {any} io
    33	 * @returns {Promise<number>}
    34	 */
    35	export async function cmdPipeline(args, io) {
    36	  /** @type {string[]} */
    37	  const positionals = [];
    38	  /** @type {string[]} */
    39	  const flagTokens = [];
    40	  for (let i = 0; i < args.length; i += 1) {
    41	    const a = args[i];
    42	    if (typeof a === 'string' && a.startsWith('--')) {
    43	      flagTokens.push(a);
    44	      if (STRING_FLAGS.has(a.slice(2)) && i + 1 < args.length) {
    45	        i += 1;
    46	        flagTokens.push(args[i]);
    47	      }
    48	    } else {
    49	      positionals.push(a);
    50	    }
    51	  }
    52	  const parsed = parseFlagsStrict(flagTokens, {});
    53	  if (parsed.error !== undefined) return usageError(io, parsed.flags, 'pipeline', parsed.error);
    54	  const flags = parsed.flags;
    55	
    56	  const sub = positionals[0];
    57	  if (sub === undefined) return usageError(io, flags, 'pipeline', 'a subcommand is required — try: baton pipeline run');
    58	  if (sub !== 'run') return usageError(io, flags, 'pipeline', `unknown subcommand '${sub}' (supported: run)`);
    59	  if (positionals.length > 1) return usageError(io, flags, 'pipeline', `unexpected argument '${positionals[1]}'`);
    60	
    61	  return runPipeline(flags, io);
    62	}
    63	
    64	/** Validate the spec's subtasks — every rejection names its offender.
    65	 * @param {any} subtasks @returns {string | null} */
    66	function validateSubtasks(subtasks) {
    67	  if (!Array.isArray(subtasks) || subtasks.length === 0) {
    68	    return 'loop.json needs a non-empty `subtasks` array ([{id, title}]) for a pipeline run';
    69	  }
    70	  const seen = new Set();
    71	  for (let i = 0; i < subtasks.length; i += 1) {
    72	    const st = subtasks[i];
    73	    if (typeof st?.id !== 'string' || st.id.length === 0) return `subtasks[${i}] is missing a string id`;
    74	    if (typeof st?.title !== 'string' || st.title.length === 0) return `subtasks[${i}] ('${st.id}') is missing a string title`;
    75	    if (seen.has(st.id)) return `subtasks carry a duplicate id '${st.id}' — ids name branches and must be unique`;
    76	    seen.add(st.id);
    77	  }
    78	  return null;
    79	}

    81	/**
    82	 * @param {Record<string, string | boolean>} flags @param {any} io
    83	 * @returns {Promise<number>}
    84	 */
    85	async function runPipeline(flags, io) {
    86	  const root = resolveRoot(io, flags);
    87	  const p = loopPaths(root);
    88	
    89	  const rawSpec = safeReadJson(io.fs, `${root}/loop.json`);

   138	    const childSpec = buildChildArgv(assignment, prompt, { root: seatPath });
   139	    return runner(childSpec, { timeoutMs, graceMs: 10_000, logPath, maxLogBytes: 1_000_000, platform: assignment.platform });
   140	  };
   141	
   142	  for (let i = 0; i < subtasks.length; i += 1) {
   143	    const st = subtasks[i];
   144	    const seat = i % 2 === 0 ? 'a' : 'b';
   145	    const other = seat === 'a' ? 'b' : 'a';
   146	    const branch = `baton/wt-${seat}/subtask-${st.id}`;
   147	    const gate = `subtask-${st.id}-review`;
   148	
   149	    const writerAssignment = resolveOne(`worker-${seat}`);
   150	    const reviewerModel = resolveOne(`worker-${other}`);
   151	    const mergerModel = resolveOne('merger');
   152	    if (!writerAssignment || !reviewerModel || !mergerModel) {
   153	      return park(`no eligible assignment for subtask '${st.id}' (worker-${seat}/worker-${other}/merger must all resolve)`);
   154	    }
   155	
   156	    // Start the subtask branch in the writer's seat, then verify the seat.
   157	    await io.execFile('git', ['checkout', '-b', branch], { cwd: seats[seat] });
   158	    const pre = await preflightWorktree(root, { seat, branch }, io);
   159	    if (pre.ok !== true) return park(`preflight refused subtask '${st.id}': ${pre.refusal}`);
   160	    const mainSha = (await io.execFile('git', ['rev-parse', 'main'], { cwd: root })).stdout.trim();
   161	
   162	    let findings = '';
   163	    let merged = false;
   164	    while (!merged) {
   165	      if ((state.iterations?.[gate] ?? 0) >= cap) {
   166	        await transition({ type: LOOP_EVENT.GATE_ITERATION, gate, verdict: 'BLOCKED' });
   167	        atomicWriteText(
   168	          io.fs,
   169	          `${p.dir}/ESCALATION.md`,
   170	          `# Pipeline escalation\n\nGate '${gate}' exhausted its ${cap}-iteration cap.\nLast findings:\n\n${findings}\n`,
   171	        );
   172	        io.stderr.write(`baton pipeline run: gate '${gate}' hit the ${cap}-iteration cap — escalated (see ${p.dir}/ESCALATION.md)\n`);
   173	        return EXIT_ESCALATED;
   174	      }
   175	
   176	      // Writer (write-capable, its own seat).
   177	      const writerPrompt = [
   178	        `You are the writer (worker-${seat}) for subtask '${st.id}': ${st.title}.`,
   179	        `Goal: ${spec.goal}. Work ONLY on branch ${branch} in your worktree; commit your work there.`,
   180	        findings ? `The previous attempt was BLOCKED — address every finding:\n${findings}` : '',
   181	      ]
   182	        .filter(Boolean)
   183	        .join('\n\n');
   184	      await spawn({ ...writerAssignment, role: `worker-${seat}` }, writerPrompt, seats[seat], `${st.id}-writer`);
   185	
   186	      const post = await postflightWorktree(root, { seat, branch, mainSha }, io);
   187	      if (post.ok !== true) return park(`postflight refused subtask '${st.id}': ${post.refusal}`);
   188	
   189	      // Reviewer (read-only, the OTHER seat's model and cwd, subtask-reviewer role).
   190	      const reviewPrompt = [
   191	        `You are the subtask-reviewer for subtask '${st.id}': ${st.title}.`,
   192	        `Review the diff of ${branch} against main from the ${other} seat (fresh context).`,
   193	        "End with exactly:\nVERDICT: APPROVED | APPROVED_WITH_NOTES | BLOCKED\nFINDINGS: numbered findings, or 'none'",
   194	      ].join('\n\n');
   195	      const review = await spawn({ ...reviewerModel, role: 'subtask-reviewer' }, reviewPrompt, seats[other], `${st.id}-review`);
   196	      if (review.verdict !== 'APPROVED' && review.verdict !== 'APPROVED_WITH_NOTES') {
   197	        findings = String(review.findings ?? '');
   198	        const after = await transition({ type: LOOP_EVENT.GATE_ITERATION, gate, verdict: 'BLOCKED' });
   199	        if (after.status === LOOP_STATUS.ESCALATED) {
   200	          atomicWriteText(io.fs, `${p.dir}/ESCALATION.md`, `# Pipeline escalation\n\nGate '${gate}' exhausted its ${cap}-iteration cap.\nLast findings:\n\n${findings}\n`);
   201	          return EXIT_ESCALATED;
   202	        }
   203	        continue;
   204	      }
   205	
   206	      // Merger (read-only child — it re-checks; the SUPERVISOR merges).
   207	      const mergerPrompt = [
   208	        `You are the merger for subtask '${st.id}'. Adversarially re-check ${branch} against main before it merges.`,
   209	        "End with exactly:\nVERDICT: APPROVED | APPROVED_WITH_NOTES | BLOCKED\nFINDINGS: numbered findings, or 'none'",
   210	      ].join('\n\n');
   211	      const mergerVerdict = await spawn({ ...mergerModel, role: 'merger' }, mergerPrompt, root, `${st.id}-merge-check`);
   212	      if (mergerVerdict.verdict !== 'APPROVED' && mergerVerdict.verdict !== 'APPROVED_WITH_NOTES') {
   213	        findings = String(mergerVerdict.findings ?? '');
   214	        const after = await transition({ type: LOOP_EVENT.GATE_ITERATION, gate, verdict: 'BLOCKED' });
   215	        if (after.status === LOOP_STATUS.ESCALATED) {
   216	          atomicWriteText(io.fs, `${p.dir}/ESCALATION.md`, `# Pipeline escalation\n\nGate '${gate}' exhausted its ${cap}-iteration cap.\nLast findings:\n\n${findings}\n`);
   217	          return EXIT_ESCALATED;
   218	        }
   219	        continue;
   220	      }
   221	
   222	      const merge = await mergeSubtask(root, { branch }, io);
   223	      if (merge.ok !== true) return park(`merge of '${branch}' refused: ${merge.reason}`);
   224	      merged = true;
   225	    }
   226	
   227	    await transition({ type: LOOP_EVENT.PHASE_ADVANCE });
   228	  }
   229	
   230	  if (flags.json) emitEnvelope(io, { ok: true, data: { status: state.status, subtasks: subtasks.length } });
   231	  else io.stdout.write(`baton pipeline run: done — ${subtasks.length} subtask(s) merged\n`);
   232	  return state.status === LOOP_STATUS.DONE ? 0 : state.status === LOOP_STATUS.PARKED ? EXIT_PARKED : 0;
   233	}

     1	/**
     2	 * Loop run state — snapshot + journal + replay for the Layer-2 supervisor,
     3	 * mirroring the bundle store's crash-tolerance idioms (atomic tmp+rename
     4	 * snapshots, torn-tail-tolerant NDJSON, seq-dedup replay). Everything lives
     5	 * under <root>/.handoff/loop/; the supervisor is the sole writer. Plan:
     6	 * docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md §"baton loop",
     7	 * §"Smoke gate", §"Supervisor lifetime and recovery", §"Caps".
     8	 */
     9	import { appendEntry, readAllTolerant } from '../util/jsonl.mjs';
    10	import { atomicWriteJson, safeReadJson, ensureDir } from '../util/fsx.mjs';
    11	import { dedupeKey } from '../util/ids.mjs';
    12	
    13	export const LOOP_STATE_SCHEMA = 'baton/loop-state@1';
    14	
    15	/** The frozen status vocabulary. */
    16	export const LOOP_STATUS = Object.freeze({
    17	  RUNNING: 'running',
    18	  AWAITING_SMOKE_APPROVAL: 'awaiting-smoke-approval',
    19	  PARKED: 'parked',
    20	  ESCALATED: 'escalated',
    21	  DONE: 'done',
    22	});
    23	
    24	/** The frozen journal event vocabulary. */
    25	export const LOOP_EVENT = Object.freeze({
    26	  PHASE_ADVANCE: 'phase-advance',
    27	  GATE_ITERATION: 'gate-iteration',
    28	  PARK: 'park',
    29	  RESUME: 'resume',
    30	  SMOKE_AWAIT: 'smoke-await',
    31	  SMOKE_APPROVE: 'smoke-approve',
    32	});
    33	
    34	// Every gate loops at most 5 times — a repo-wide hard invariant (AGENTS.md
    35	// §Review gates); the reducer refuses a 6th and escalates instead.
    36	const GATE_ITERATION_CAP = 5;
    37	
    38	/** @param {string} root */
    39	export function loopPaths(root) {
    40	  const dir = `${root}/.handoff/loop`;
    41	  return { dir, state: `${dir}/state.json`, journal: `${dir}/journal.ndjson` };
    42	}
    43	
    44	/**
    45	 * Fresh run state — pure and deterministic from the injected io (identity
    46	 * facts + clock only; no Date.now/Math.random, which would break replay).
    47	 * @param {any} spec a VALIDATED loop spec @param {any} io
    48	 */
    49	export function initLoopState(spec, io) {
    50	  const runId = `loop-${dedupeKey({ host: io.host, pid: io.pid, startTime: io.startTime, at: io.now() }).slice(0, 12)}`;
    51	  /** @type {Record<string, number>} */
    52	  const iterations = {};
    53	  for (const p of spec.phases ?? []) {
    54	    if (/gate/.test(String(p.id))) iterations[p.id] = 0;
    55	  }
    56	  return {
    57	    schema: LOOP_STATE_SCHEMA,
    58	    runId,
    59	    goal: spec.goal,
    60	    phaseCount: Array.isArray(spec.phases) ? spec.phases.length : 0,
    61	    phaseIndex: 0,
    62	    iterations,
    63	    status: LOOP_STATUS.RUNNING,
    64	    parkReason: null,
    65	    escalation: null,
    66	    smokeApproval: null,
    67	    createdAt: io.now(),
    68	    journalSeq: 0,
    69	  };
    70	}
    71	
    72	/**
    73	 * The pure transition reducer. Never mutates its input; unknown events and
    74	 * refused transitions return the input state unchanged (a new reference is
    75	 * only produced for a real transition).
    76	 * @param {any} state @param {any} ev
    77	 */
    78	export function applyLoopEvent(state, ev) {
    79	  switch (ev?.type) {
    80	    case LOOP_EVENT.PHASE_ADVANCE: {
    81	      const next = state.phaseIndex + 1;
    82	      if (next >= state.phaseCount) return { ...state, phaseIndex: state.phaseCount, status: LOOP_STATUS.DONE };
    83	      return { ...state, phaseIndex: next };
    84	    }
    85	    case LOOP_EVENT.GATE_ITERATION: {
    86	      const gate = String(ev.gate);
    87	      const current = state.iterations?.[gate] ?? 0;
    88	      // The 6th attempt is REFUSED: the counter stays at the cap and the run
    89	      // escalates with a record naming the gate — never a 6th run.
    90	      if (current >= GATE_ITERATION_CAP) {
    91	        return { ...state, status: LOOP_STATUS.ESCALATED, escalation: { gate, iteration: current } };
    92	      }
    93	      return { ...state, iterations: { ...state.iterations, [gate]: current + 1 } };
    94	    }
    95	    case LOOP_EVENT.PARK:
    96	      return { ...state, status: LOOP_STATUS.PARKED, parkReason: ev.reason ?? null };
    97	    case LOOP_EVENT.RESUME:
    98	      // Only a PARK is resumable; an escalation is an operator decision.
    99	      if (state.status !== LOOP_STATUS.PARKED) return state;
   100	      return { ...state, status: LOOP_STATUS.RUNNING, parkReason: null };
   101	    case LOOP_EVENT.SMOKE_AWAIT:
   102	      return { ...state, status: LOOP_STATUS.AWAITING_SMOKE_APPROVAL };
   103	    case LOOP_EVENT.SMOKE_APPROVE: {
   104	      // Guarded transition: only meaningful while awaiting, and only with the
   105	      // caller's verifySmokeToken result stamped verified === true. A missing
   106	      // or false `verified` is unverified — it never resumes the run.
   107	      if (state.status !== LOOP_STATUS.AWAITING_SMOKE_APPROVAL) return state;
   108	      if (ev.verified !== true) return state;
   109	      return { ...state, status: LOOP_STATUS.RUNNING, smokeApproval: { token: ev.token, at: ev.ts ?? null } };
   110	    }
   111	    default:
   112	      return state;
   113	  }
   114	}
   115	
   116	/**
   117	 * Atomic snapshot write (tmp + rename under .handoff/loop).
   118	 * @param {string} root @param {any} state @param {any} io
   119	 */
   120	export async function writeLoopState(root, state, io) {
   121	  const p = loopPaths(root);
   122	  ensureDir(io.fs, p.dir);
   123	  atomicWriteJson(io.fs, p.state, state);
   124	}
   125	
   126	/**
   127	 * Append one journal event; allocates the next seq past
   128	 * max(snapshot.journalSeq, journal tail) unless the entry carries one.
   129	 * @param {string} root @param {any} ev @param {any} io
   130	 * @returns {Promise<number>} the persisted seq
   131	 */
   132	export async function appendLoopEvent(root, ev, io) {
   133	  const p = loopPaths(root);
   134	  ensureDir(io.fs, p.dir);
   135	  let seq = ev.seq;
   136	  if (typeof seq !== 'number') {
   137	    const snap = safeReadJson(io.fs, p.state);
   138	    const snapSeq = snap.ok && typeof snap.value?.journalSeq === 'number' ? snap.value.journalSeq : 0;
   139	    let tailSeq = 0;
   140	    if (io.fs.existsSync(p.journal)) {
   141	      for (const e of readAllTolerant(io.fs, p.journal).entries) {
   142	        if (typeof e.seq === 'number' && e.seq > tailSeq) tailSeq = e.seq;
   143	      }
   144	    }
   145	    seq = Math.max(snapSeq, tailSeq) + 1;
   146	  }
   147	  const ts = typeof ev.ts === 'string' ? ev.ts : io.now();
   148	  const entry = {
   149	    ...ev,
   150	    seq,
   151	    ts,
   152	    dedupeKey: typeof ev.dedupeKey === 'string' && ev.dedupeKey.length > 0 ? ev.dedupeKey : dedupeKey({ seq, ts, type: ev.type, gate: ev.gate ?? null }),
   153	  };
   154	  appendEntry(io.fs, p.journal, entry);
   155	  return seq;
   156	}
   157	
   158	/**
   159	 * Load = snapshot + torn-tail-tolerant replay of journal events PAST the
   160	 * snapshot's seq. Duplicate seqs/dedupeKeys apply exactly once, so a
   161	 * supervisor-death recovery never re-runs a completed iteration.
   162	 * @param {string} root @param {any} io
   163	 * @returns {Promise<{state: any, warnings: string[]}>}
   164	 */
   165	export async function loadLoopState(root, io) {
   166	  const p = loopPaths(root);
   167	  /** @type {string[]} */
   168	  const warnings = [];
   169	  const snap = safeReadJson(io.fs, p.state);
   170	  if (!snap.ok) return { state: null, warnings: ['no loop state snapshot found'] };
   171	  let state = snap.value;
   172	  const baseSeq = typeof state.journalSeq === 'number' ? state.journalSeq : 0;
   173	
   174	  if (io.fs.existsSync(p.journal)) {
   175	    const { entries, warnings: jw } = readAllTolerant(io.fs, p.journal);
   176	    for (const w of jw ?? []) warnings.push(`journal: ${w.kind ?? 'warning'} at line ${w.line ?? '?'}`);
   177	    const applied = new Set();
   178	    let maxSeq = baseSeq;
   179	    for (const e of entries) {
   180	      const seq = typeof e.seq === 'number' ? e.seq : null;
   181	      if (seq !== null && seq <= baseSeq) continue; // already folded into the snapshot
   182	      const key = e.dedupeKey ?? (seq !== null ? `seq:${seq}` : null);
   183	      if (key !== null && applied.has(key)) continue; // replayed duplicate — apply once
   184	      if (seq !== null && applied.has(`seq:${seq}`)) continue;
   185	      state = applyLoopEvent(state, e);
   186	      if (key !== null) applied.add(key);
   187	      if (seq !== null) {
   188	        applied.add(`seq:${seq}`);
   189	        if (seq > maxSeq) maxSeq = seq;
   190	      }
   191	    }
   192	    state = { ...state, journalSeq: maxSeq };
   193	  }
   194	  return { state, warnings };
   195	}
   196	
   197	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   198	// Smoke-approval token — a STRUCTURED digest over the six bound inputs, so a
   199	// stale approval is refused NAMING what moved (mirrors the receive receipt
   200	// token's driftedInputs discipline).
   201	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   202	
   203	const SMOKE_TOKEN_PREFIX = 'smk1';
   204	const SMOKE_TOKEN_FIELDS = /** @type {const} */ ([
   205	  'stateDigest',
   206	  'smokeCmd',
   207	  'smokeOutputDigest',
   208	  'gitHead',
   209	  'gitContentDigest',
   210	  'childAssignment',
   211	]);
   212	
   213	/**
   214	 * Deterministic structured token. Per-field digests keep field boundaries
   215	 * (no concatenation collisions) and let verification name drifted inputs.
   216	 * Missing bound fields throw — a token over partial inputs is meaningless.
   217	 * @param {Record<string, string>} inputs
   218	 * @returns {string}
   219	 */
   220	export function smokeApprovalToken(inputs) {

   110	const gitContentOf = (g) => g?.contentDigest ?? dedupeKey(g ?? null);

   112	/**
   113	 * The supervisor state machine: drives the spec's phases through headless
   114	 * role children, records every transition in the journal-replayable loop
   115	 * state, enforces the 5-cap, pauses at the smoke gate, and hands limit
   116	 * deaths to the failover transaction. Children run via the injectable seam
   117	 * `io.superviseChild ?? superviseChild`.
   118	 * @param {Record<string, string | boolean>} flags @param {any} io
   119	 * @returns {Promise<number>}
   120	 */
   121	async function runLoop(flags, io) {
   122	  const root = resolveRoot(io, flags);
   123	  const specPath = `${root}/loop.json`;
   124	  const p = loopPaths(root);
   125	  const lockPath = `${p.dir}/supervisor.lock`;
   126	
   127	  // Preconditions — before any lock or spawn.
   128	  const rawSpec = safeReadJson(io.fs, specPath);
   129	  if (!rawSpec.ok) {
   130	    return usageError(io, flags, 'loop', `no loop.json at ${specPath} — scaffold one with: baton loop init "<goal>"`);
   131	  }
   132	  const { config } = loadConfig(root, io);
   133	  if (!config) return usageError(io, flags, 'loop', `no readable baton.config.json at ${root} — the role matrix is required`);
   134	  const validated = validateLoopSpec(rawSpec.value, config);
   135	  if (validated.ok !== true) {
   136	    return usageError(io, flags, 'loop', `loop.json is invalid:\n  - ${validated.errors.join('\n  - ')}`);
   137	  }
   138	  const spec = validated.spec;
   139	
   140	  // Supervisor run lock — same provably-dead discipline as the bundle lock:
   141	  // a live same-host owner is refused; a dead one is reclaimed.
   142	  const existingLock = safeReadJson(io.fs, lockPath);
   143	  if (existingLock.ok && existingLock.value && typeof existingLock.value.pid === 'number') {
   144	    const other = existingLock.value;
   145	    const alive = typeof io.processAlive === 'function' ? io.processAlive(other.pid) : true;
   146	    const sameProcess = other.pid === io.pid && other.startTime === io.startTime;
   147	    if (!sameProcess && (other.host !== io.host || alive)) {
   148	      io.stderr.write(`baton loop run: another supervisor (pid ${other.pid} on ${other.host}, run ${other.runId}) holds the run lock — refusing a second supervisor\n`);
   149	      return 1;
   150	    }
   151	    io.stderr.write(`baton loop run: reclaimed the run lock from a provably-dead supervisor (pid ${other.pid})\n`);
   152	  }
   153	
   154	  // Load or initialize the run state.
   155	  let { state } = await loadLoopState(root, io);
   156	  if (state === null) {
   157	    state = initLoopState(spec, io);
